  + Affine base word b_0 (27 letters) is reduced of length 27, an involution, has right descents {1,3,5,7,8}, is two-sided terminal, its matrix equals B_0 in Appendix B, d=(2,-1,1,-1,1,-1,1,-1,-1), (delta,delta)=(gamma,delta)=0, (gamma,gamma)=2, and letter deletion yields exactly 21 distinct length-26 covers — checks.py.
  + Length formula 27+92k follows from the (A,B) multiplicity table: 44k+16k+1+20(1+k)+6(1+2k)=27+92k; 44+8+1+20+6=79 and 240-79=161 — arithmetic.
  + E10 lowering sequence (50 steps) reduces beta_0=(3,7,10,9,7,6,4,3,1,6) to alpha_9 through nonnegative vectors with strictly decreasing height (56 to 1); consistent with the stated base length 101 = 2*51-1 — checks.py.
  + Lowering sequences for gamma and gamma+delta in Appendix B end at the branch simple root for r=3, r=4 and for the E10 embedding — checks.py.
  + beta_3=(2,3,5,5,4,4,3,3,2,2,1,1,3) matches the general seed formula; pairings m_j of the seed for r=3,4,5 match the k=0 case of the formulas in Lemma 12; (beta_r,gamma)=-r and (beta_r,delta)=-a; beta_{r,k,0}=r-1+4k+(4r-8)k^2; all edge sums in Lemma 12 nonpositive — hand algebra.
  + E10 pairings (-1,1,-2-k,1+k,...,-1-k^2-3k,2+k), (beta_0,gamma)=-3, (beta_0,delta)=-1, beta_{k,0}=2k^2+5k+3 — hand algebra.
  + Lemma 10 orthonormal realization: (alpha_0,alpha_0)=2, (alpha_0,alpha_1)=-1, (alpha_0,alpha_j)=0 otherwise; s_0 beta_r = F alpha_0 — hand algebra.
  + D4 example in Section 6: P_{x,xcx}=1+2q with ell(x)=3, ell(b)=7 — full KL recursion over all 192 elements of D4, checks.py; Mongelli's Corollary 3 (tree-Coxeter groups, Boolean u,v) does apply since xcx is a Boolean reflection.
  + Gern's thesis uses exactly the terminology quoted (Definition 2.1.1: 'bad' and 'weakly bad'), Theorem 4.5.11 is the type-D mu in {0,1} theorem, Lemma 4.5.5 gives mu(x_6,w_6)=1 (computed with du Cloux's Coxeter), Example 1.1.6 is the signed-permutation model of D_n — sources/gern-thesis-2013.txt lines 118, 175-200, 627-630, 3439-3441, 3501.
  + All 16 file paths named in Appendix A exist in the repository; GitHub release v0.1.0 exists with assets exceptional-leading.pdf, exceptional-leading-proof.zip and SHA256SUMS.
  + Abstract claims match the body (Theorem 1, Corollary 2, Theorem 3, Theorem 13, Proposition 14); the terminal counts 1/4/6 in the introduction match Table 1; odd gaps in Table 1 occur only in the two length-15 rows.
  + README.md and supplement/README.md numbers agree with the manuscript: 11 table rows, 2,903,040 E7 elements, D6 polynomial 1+6q+11q^2+6q^3+q^4+q^5, E6/E7/E8/E9 catalogues, --full flag semantics.
  + LaTeX build log (tmp/pdfs/build/exceptional-leading/exceptional-leading.log) contains no overfull/underfull boxes, undefined references or citations; all \ref/\eqref targets resolve in the renders.

UNRESOLVED:
  ? Laird's M.S. thesis is not in the repository, so I could not confirm that §5.3 lists the type-E_n classification as a future direction (line 170-171).
  ? Shi, Proposition 2.10 (star operations preserve full commutativity in simply laced type) was not checked against the source; not in the repository.
  ? Green's Proposition 4.4(iv),(v) and Definition 4.1 in arXiv:0704.0283 were not re-read for this presentation review; the correctness dimension should confirm the paraphrase in Lemma 5.
  ? Whether the published version of Mongelli's paper numbers the relevant corollary differently from the arXiv version (the manuscript writes 'preprint Corollary 3'); only the arXiv text is in the repository, where Corollary 3 is the tree-Coxeter Boolean formula.

==================== reproducibility ====================
SUMMARY:
The computational supplement is, in practice, reproducible. I copied output/supplement/exceptional-leading-proof.zip to a scratch directory, confirmed its SHA-256 (595d932c...8861b4) against output/supplement/SHA256SUMS, output/supplement/build-summary.json and supplement/dist/build-summary.json, extracted it, and ran `python3 run_proofs.py` with Python 3.14.7 and Apple clang 21. The default mode completed all 24 steps in 12.5 s wall-clock (186 MB peak RSS), exit status 0, and the generated summary.json is byte-for-byte equal to expected-summary.json; the runner's extra check that the eleven printed table words equal the regenerated terminal matrices also passed. Because the default was fast and the machine has 24 GB, I also ran `--full`: 30 steps, 16.0 s, 518 MB peak RSS, exit 0, summary again identical to expected-summary.json, and the regenerated E7 root-index snapshot and both E6/E7 integer-matrix snapshots are byte-identical to the shipped ones. In a copy of the repository (without .git, tmp, output), `computations/run_search.py` (3.2 s) and `computations/verify_certificate.py` (2.1 s) both exited 0; the regenerated results/d6-recurrence-certificate.json is byte-identical to the committed one, and results/computation.json differs only in elapsed_seconds and an embedded absolute path.

I also wrote my own exact-integer verifier from the manuscript's stated conventions (scratchpad/reproducibility/independent_check.py) and confirmed, without using any project code: all eleven Table 1 words are reduced, have the stated lengths, L=R=supp(x), pass the two-sided terminal test, are not FC, and the independence numbers are as stated; the D7 weight (4,7,10,8,6,4,2,5) has pairings (1,0,...,0) and an orbit of exactly 2,160; the E6/E7/E8 Poincaré products from the exponents give 51,840/2,903,040/696,729,600 with maximal lengths 36/63/120 and indices 27, 56, 240, 2,160; the root counts 72/126/240; the B_0 matrix of Appendix B, b_0^2=1, the d vector, the (A,B) multiplicity table 44/8/1/20/6 with 161 identically zero contributions, the 21 non-FC covers, and the lengths 27, 119, 211, 303 for k=0..3 obtained by descent-stripping (independent of the inversion formula); and in E10 the 50-step lowering witness, the pairing formula for beta_k, base length 101 by descent stripping, and terminality for k=0..4. The FC counts 662/2,670/10,846/44,199 (and the repo's 180,438 for E10) agree with Stembridge's 1996 Table 1. Every number listed in the task is backed by a certificate in results/, research/ or expected-summary.json and agrees with it.

The weaknesses I found are provenance and documentation issues, not mathematical ones. (1) The manuscript's Appendix A table names `research/en_e8/reproduce.py` as a path "relative to payload/", but that file is not in the supplement at all (it exists only in the repository and would write into the source tree). (2) The shipped E6 "historical root-index snapshot" research/broad_exceptional/e6_bad.json is the output of exact_e.py with a hand-added `"commuting_weak": 22` key, not the output of enumerate_bad.cpp which INPUTS.json says rebuilds it; `--full` silently overwrites it with a differently structured file and never compares the two, and verify_exceptional.py contains schema-tolerant code to paper over this. (3) The committed research/en_uniform/referee-certificate.json (and referee.txt) record source hashes of older manuscript versions, and results/e8-independent-audit.json hashes a file containing a volatile timing field, so several committed certificates are not reproducible byte-for-byte and are stale relative to the committed manuscript (the supplement regenerates them, so this does not affect the archive). The independence claims in supplement/README.md are stated carefully and are accurate as far as the import graph goes; the D7 engine is a separate program but uses the same packed root-index representation and essentially the same algorithmic skeleton as the flat engine, so "separate implementation" is fair while "independent" would overstate it. A journal referee with Python 3.10+ and a C++17 compiler can realistically verify every computational claim of the paper from the archive alone in under a minute, and Appendix A otherwise describes the runner accurately.

I did not independently re-implement the E6/E7/E8 terminal classification (completeness of the terminal lists); I verified that the listed terminals have the claimed properties and that the authors' three programs agree, and I reran all of them. ==================== secondary-manuscripts ====================
SUMMARY:
Both secondary notes are mathematically sound as far as I can determine. I re-derived every stated proof in results/kl-transfer.tex (witness certificate, obstruction, I2(3)/I2(4) FC example, bad-interval degree theorem and its two gap corollaries, tensor-product theorem, eight-element dihedral obstruction, convex-coset lemma and classical coset KL equality, affine translation example, E7/E8 coset example) and in results/star-boundary.tex (reducedness of a_L c a_R, distinctness, cube face lattice, R_{x,z}=(q-1)^{|L∩R|+1}, the reciprocity derivation of the Catalan-triangle formula) and found no logical gaps. Their description of the OpenAI preprint's Theorem 1.1 is accurate: the preprint states it for arbitrary Coxeter systems (W,S),(W',S'), for a poset isomorphism between whole closed intervals in possibly different groups, unconditionally and including infinite and noncrystallographic cases (sources/combinatorial-invariance-2026.txt lines 65-77). The notes correctly treat it as an external input rather than a verified theorem.

I independently reproduced, with fresh exact-integer code in my scratchpad, all the computational claims I could reach: all six interval certificates in results/computation.json (bijections are genuine order isomorphisms, polynomials, mu-values, rank vectors, FC statuses, reduced words, and the order-4 cover-reflection obstruction), the D6 polynomial P_{x6,w6}=1+6q+11q^2+6q^3+q^4+q^5 with all four right descents and R-reciprocity, the I6 shape facts (1676 vertices, 3184-element ideal, rank vector, 12 atoms, the non-lattice witness c,d with 720 common upper bounds), Lemma 2.3.9 (P_{e,w6}=P_{x6,w6}), the thesis length/a-value/degree table for n=4..24, the star family for m=1..6 (including affine D4: |[e,w]|=272, |[x,w]|=82, rank counts (1,16,32,24,8,1), P=1+3q+2q^2, mu=2, and the stated affine actions of x and w), the Boolean-face R/P claims for m<=6, the E7/E8 coset lengths (33/90, 37/48, 94/105) and non-FC witnesses, the absence of 8-element rank-4 intervals in A5, D4, D5, and the coset lemma/proposition in D4 and B3. Literature locators that I could check (Gern Thm 4.5.11, Def 2.3.8, Cor 2.2.19, Lemma 2.2.20, Prop 4.5.1, Lemma 2.3.9, Lemmas 4.5.2-4.5.7, Prop 1.3.5 citing Lusztig 1.3(a), Prop 1.2.6, Lemma 1.1.21/Cor 1.1.22; Mongelli Corollary 2 on p.10 and f_4 on p.6; Green arXiv Thm 3.1 / published Thm 4.1 and the "possibly all of them" sentence; Green Remark 7.13; Jones Thm 1.2; Woo arXiv Cor 2; BGL rank<=6; EMS rank 10/12; Chen 1804.02353) are accurate.

The weaknesses are editorial and about repository hygiene rather than mathematics. star-boundary.tex is stale relative to the main manuscript (it still says the companion theorem covers E6/E7 only). The root-level kl-transfer.pdf is an untracked, outdated duplicate missing the "Update" paragraph. Neither note has a human author or affiliation, neither is linked from README.md, and the kl-transfer "Update from the expanded search" paragraph reads as a status log rather than a mathematical text. A few citations are imprecise (Green's affine-A result phrased as if finite A were primary; Brenti corollary numbering attached to an FPSAC extended-abstract link; Green Remark 7.13 is a remark, not a theorem). The novelty disclaimers are, if anything, overcautious and accurate: the notes claim only conditional deductions and explicitly label the star family and the coset examples as classical/prior art, which I confirmed (Mongelli Corollary 2 is exactly the star pattern with Catalan mu).

I consulted the pre-existing AI referee/editorial reports only at the very end via a grep for mentions of the two notes; they essentially do not discuss these documents, so nothing from them changed my assessment.

  + star-boundary prior-art claim: Mongelli's boolean elements are elements below a boolean reflection (preprint lines 187-191), so x and w=xcx qualify; Corollary 2 (p.10, confirmed in mongelli-p10.png) is exactly the star pattern with mu=C([(h+1)/2]); f_4=2q^2+3q+1 is displayed on p.6; Green's 'possibly all of them' sentence is in the introduction (pp.2-3) and is not a numbered conjecture.
  + Consistency with computations/README.md: six certificates (one B3, three B4, two A4), D4-to-B4 transfer with P=1+2q+q^2 and rank vector (1,4,10,12,6,1), order-4 cover reflections, D6 base calculation, B2 absence test (966 D4 intervals, 0 models) all consistent with the notes; main manuscript Section 'Scope' and D4 P_{x,b}=1+2q (m=3 star) consistent with both notes.
  + Build state: sha256 of results/kl-transfer.tex and results/star-boundary.tex match results/pdf-build-manifest.json; both PDFs render cleanly (pages inspected: kl-transfer-01, kl-transfer-04, star-boundary-1).

UNRESOLVED:
  ? Chmutov (2014) Theorem 2.3.8 (type B FC-bottom bound) could not be checked: deepblue.lib.umich.edu returned 403; the project's research/literature.txt reports verifying it through indexed text and a ResearchGate copy.
  ? Dyer (1991) 'Lemma 3.1 and Section 3.4' as antecedent of the reflection-plane argument (kl-transfer.tex line 399) was not checked against the Compositio paper.
  ? Brenti Invent. Math. 118 (1994) 'Corollaries 6.8-6.9' numbering (star-boundary.tex) could not be verified; the linked FPSAC PDF is image-only.
  ? The full proof of the OpenAI combinatorial-invariance preprint was not audited (nor do the notes claim to audit it); all transfer statements remain conditional on it.
  ? The D8 value mu(x_8,w_8)=0 is inherited from Gern's Lemma 4.5.5 (du Cloux's coxeter); I did not recompute it, consistent with the note's own disclaimer.
  ? Green's mu-tilde in Remark 7.13 of the Jones-traces paper: I did not check how it relates to the ordinary mu.

==================== integrity-hygiene ====================
SUMMARY:
The repository is already public: the GitHub API reports github.com/tygern/kl as visibility "public", license null, created 2026-10-07T12:37Z, and release v0.1.0 (PDF, proof ZIP, SHA256SUMS) was published at 12:46Z the same day. So the questions in this dimension are not "what to do before release" but "what must be fixed in an artifact that is already distributed". Git hygiene is better than the task premise suggested: the tmp/pdfs PNGs, __pycache__, the enumerate_bad Mach-O binary, the 14.9 MB e10-fc.json, all logs, the duplicate ZIPs and the stale root kl-transfer.pdf are all gitignored and untracked (215 tracked files, 2.3 MiB; the only binaries tracked are the three output/pdf PDFs). The working tree, however, carries ~60 MB of ignored clutter and the .gitignore relies on named exceptions rather than generic rules.

The substantive integrity problems are about disclosure and attribution. The manuscript results/exceptional-leading.tex contains no acknowledgement, methods or disclosure statement of any kind about AI involvement, although prompts/01-03.txt show the entire pipeline (find, prove, verify, cite, write up) was delegated to "Astra subagents", and research/editorial/revision-summary.txt item 6 records that "agent/model history" was deliberately removed from the article. Every policy I checked (arXiv moderation policy, AMS "Use of Artificial Intelligence", Algebraic Combinatorics journal policy, Springer Nature AI-in-manuscript-preparation) requires that such use be disclosed in the paper and that a human author take responsibility; all forbid AI as an author. Yet \author{} is empty, the PDF /Author field is empty, the supplement README says "no author identity is assigned", while the companion kl-transfer.pdf (tracked, public) literally sets \author{Research draft prepared with an Astra research team}. These must be reconciled by a named human author plus an explicit AI-use statement before any arXiv or journal submission.

The internal AI review files are honestly caveated in a few places (README: "not journal decisions"; response-to-referees.txt: "simulated specialist reports"), but the file headers say "Independent correctness referee report", "INDEPENDENT SPECIALIST REFEREE REPORT", "Recommendation: MINOR REVISION", "This editorial approval", and one of them (research/en_uniform/referee.txt, headed "INDEPENDENT REFEREE") ships inside the public proof ZIP with no indication it is machine-generated. "Independent" is not accurate for reviews commissioned from the same AI system by the same workflow; the wording should be changed to "AI-generated internal review notes" and a one-line banner added to each file.

Hygiene findings that should be fixed before the next release: 6 tracked files contain /Users/ic/... paths and 18 contain /private/tmp/... paths (five of those source files are inside the public ZIP); the manuscript's reproduction table names research/en_e8/reproduce.py "relative to payload/" but the ZIP has no such file; there is no LICENSE anywhere (repo or ZIP), which under default copyright leaves the public code view-and-fork only; the prompts that document provenance are gitignored; and history was squashed to one commit so the hashes recorded in the various manifests cannot be checked out. Consistency between README, release notes and manuscript is otherwise good; one wording nit is publication.json's "published_and_verified", which denotes only checksum matching.

I consulted the existing AI reports only at the end. They had noted the absence of a license and author identity in passing (correctness.txt:32, followup-response.txt:24, supplement/REPORT.txt:39) but none raised the missing AI-use disclosure in the manuscript, the kl-transfer AI author line, the "Independent referee" labelling, the local-path leaks, or the missing reproduce.py in the ZIP.

==================== ci-preprint-usage ====================
SUMMARY:
What the preprint states. sources/combinatorial-invariance-2026.txt (OpenAI, 24 Sept 2026), Theorem 1.1 (lines 65-76): "Let (W,S) and (W',S') be arbitrary Coxeter systems, and let u ≤ b in W and u' ≤ b' in W'. If ι:[u,b] → [u',b'] is an isomorphism of abstract Bruhat interval posets, then ... P^W_{u,b}(q) = P^{W'}_{u',b'}(q)." It adds: "The statement includes infinite and noncrystallographic groups." So: all Coxeter systems (including reducible, infinite, non-crystallographic), all Bruhat intervals (not only lower intervals), across different Coxeter systems, equal-parameter P-polynomials; Corollary 6.1 (line 1620) extends to all subintervals and, via reciprocity, to R-polynomials; Theorem 3.4 (line 631) reconstructs the directed Bruhat graph. The only hypothesis is a poset isomorphism of the closed intervals; the argument is presented as complete and unconditional ("We prove ...", "establishing Theorem 1.1", line 1618). The preprint itself attributes the conjecture to Lusztig and Dyer and surveys Brenti, BCM, Delanoy, du Cloux, Incitti, Patimo, Barkley-Gaetz(-Lam), BBDVW, EMS, Caselli-Marietti (lines 84-120); its cited arXiv IDs 2601.07793v2, 2509.16433v2, 2606.11776v1 resolve to the stated papers.

Logical use in the repo. Every deduction I checked is within the theorem's hypotheses. In results/exceptional-leading.tex §Scope (lines 682-690) the transfer "an interval isomorphic to a finite simply laced FC-bottom interval has µ ∈ {0,1}" needs only that a poset isomorphism of Bruhat intervals preserves rank (hence the parity of ℓ(w)-ℓ(x) and cover status) and P; both follow from Theorem 1.1, and Corollary cor:finiteADE (line 73) is stated for all finite simply laced W, reducible included. "The target bottom need not be FC" is correct: Theorem 1.1 imposes no condition on the target endpoints. results/kl-transfer.tex: Theorem 1.3 (witness certificate), Corollary 1.4, Theorem 2.1 (bad intervals; arithmetic of L_n, d_n, a_n, M_n, A_n and the table re-derived exactly), Corollary 2.2 (gap formulas re-derived for n=8k and n=8k+6), Proposition 2.3, Theorem 2.4 (product intervals live in the reducible product Coxeter system, which Theorem 1.1 covers) all invoke exactly a whole-closed-interval isomorphism and nothing more. The thesis locators used (Def. 2.3.8, Lemma 2.3.9, Prop. 1.3.5, Prop. 4.5.1, Lemmas 4.5.2-4.5.6) were checked against sources/gern-thesis-2013.txt and support µ(x_n,w_n)=1 iff n=6 for even n. results/star-boundary.tex correctly says it does not use the theorem. The conditional framing ("Assuming ...", "Assume the invariance input", "we do not independently certify its full proof", README's "separate conditional observation") is logically consistent across documents.

Where the documents fall short is status disclosure and history. (1) The repo describes the source as "the combinatorial-invariance theorem of [CI]" / "supplied preprint" and never records what the source actually is: an AI-generated manuscript hosted only on GitHub (not on arXiv), with no named human author, in a repository whose README says "Some of the unformalized results could have issues," and whose sole Lean artifact for this result (lean/ComparatorChallenges/KLInvariance.lean) states the theorem and ends in `sorry`. The kisonecat index the authors' literature notes cite advertises "Lean notes" for the paper, which could be mistaken for machine verification; it is not. I found no erratum, no GitHub issue, no arXiv version and no public expert discussion of this specific preprint as of 7 Oct 2026, so the status is simply "unreviewed claim." (2) Neither manuscript attributes the conjecture to Lusztig and Dyer or cites the prior partial results in the main paper; kl-transfer cites a subset (BCM, Delanoy, BGL, EMS, Dyer 1991) and omits Brenti 2004, du Cloux, Incitti, Patimo, BBDVW/Davies et al., Barkley-Gaetz, Brenti-Marietti, Gurevich-Wang, Caselli-Marietti. For a paper that invokes the theorem by name in a Scope section this is a real citation gap, though local to fix.

Overall for this dimension: no critical or major finding. The transfer statements are correct formal consequences of the stated theorem and are properly flagged as conditional; the needed fixes are to state the preprint's provenance/verification status accurately and uniformly, to attribute the conjecture and its history, and small housekeeping (stale root-level kl-transfer.pdf, tool-name author field).

FINDINGS:

  + kl-transfer Proposition 4.1 (B2 principal interval has no finite simply laced realization): the reflection-plane argument is sound (rank-two subsystems of a finite simply laced root system are A1×A1 or A2, reflection group of order ≤6 < 8) and the note correctly labels it classical and independent of Theorem 1.1.
  + kl-transfer §5 coset lemma/proposition and E7/E8 coset lengths (w0 lengths 63, 120; D6 length 30; a of length 33 and 90; endpoints (37,48), (94,105)) are arithmetically correct and correctly described as classical, not requiring Theorem 1.1.
  + kl-transfer literature table scopes for Barkley–Gaetz–Lam (full invariance for length ≤ 6, arXiv 2601.07793 v2) and Esposito–Marietti–Stella (length ≤ 10 Weyl, ≤ 12 when both realizations are type A, arXiv 2509.16433 v2) match the arXiv abstracts fetched 2026-10-07.
  + star-boundary.tex: correctly states it does not use the invariance theorem (abstract and §4); its content is unaffected by the preprint.
  + README.md:14 'Combinatorial-invariance transfer is a separate conditional observation' and output/supplement/release-notes.md (no CI claim) are consistent with the conditional framing in the manuscripts.
  + sources/manifest.json records the preprint's URL and SHA-256 and notes 'not a guarantee of external acceptance'; research/literature.txt item 15 (lines 218-225) correctly instructs conditional description.
  + The three 2026 arXiv references in the preprint's bibliography (2601.07793, 2509.16433, 2606.11776) exist and have the stated titles/authors (fetched arXiv abstracts).
  + The openai/math repository's Lean artifact for this preprint (lean/ComparatorChallenges/KLInvariance.lean) formalizes only the statement; the main theorem is `sorry`. No GitHub issue, erratum, or revision of paper.pdf was found (single 'Initial commit' on 6 Oct 2026).

UNRESOLVED:
  ? I did not audit the preprint's proof (Sections 2-6) for correctness; that is outside this dimension and would require a dedicated review. Its reliance on an unpublished Dyer manuscript ([19]) could not be checked.
  ? No public expert commentary, erratum or arXiv posting specific to the OpenAI combinatorial-invariance preprint was found via web search as of 7 Oct 2026; absence of discussion is not evidence of correctness or error.
  ? The task listed 'Burrull-Gurevich-Wang' among prior literature; I could not locate a joint paper by these three authors. The Math. Z. 2024 paper 'Kazhdan–Lusztig R-polynomials, combinatorial invariance, and hypercube decompositions' is by Brenti–Marietti, and Gurevich–Wang's Selecta 2024 paper is 'Parabolic recursions for Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials and the hypercube decomposition'. Springer pages were behind a redirect so volumes/pages were not confirmed.
  ? Theorem numbers for Brenti–Caselli–Marietti (Thm 7.8, Cor 8.4) and Delanoy (Thm 7.2, Cor 7.3) quoted in kl-transfer were not independently verified against those papers.
  ? The D6 recurrence certificate (Proposition 2.3 of kl-transfer) and the B3/B4/A4 interval certificates were not re-run here; they belong to the computational dimension.
  ? Final cross-check against the repo's own referee/editorial reports (research/journal-review/correctness.txt:35, significance.txt:43, research/editorial/hierarchy.txt:41, style.txt:209-211): they treat the transfer as logically correct and conditional, as I do; none records the `sorry`-only Lean status or the missing Lusztig–Dyer attribution, so findings D1 and D2 are additional to them. Consulting them did not change my findings.

==================== followup-fan-primary-source ====================
TASK: Obtain C. K. Fan, 'Structure of a Hecke algebra quotient', J. Amer. Math. Soc. 10 (1997) 139-167 (free at ams.org/jams) and verify, for the E_n family with n >= 6 including affine E9 and indefinite E10+, the statements Green attributes to it in arXiv:0704.0283 Proposition 4.4: Definition 2.3.1 (a-function), Proposition 4.4.3 (right cell of i(A) = {w = i(A)x reduced, a(w) = #A}) and Theorem 6.1.2 (whose proof is claimed to give the singleton intersection of a left and a right monomial cell in one two-sided cell). Check Fan's standing hypotheses (which Coxeter graphs, which base ring, whether 'cells' are the monomial-basis cells Green uses) and that nothing restricts to finite or FC-finite type. Also check the left-hand version of 4.4(iv) that Lemma maxI invokes. Report with page/theorem numbers.
SUMMARY:
I obtained Fan's primary source (C. K. Fan, Structure of a Hecke algebra quotient, JAMS 10 (1997) 139-167, retrieved from ams.org, sha256 500ef950...dde2, text extracted with pdftotext into my scratch directory) and also Fan's 1996 JACO paper, which the JAMS appendix cites for Lemma 8.1.1. Fan's standing hypothesis (p. 139, Section 1) is that the Coxeter graph is of type A, D or E, "where by E, we mean the infinite series E_n which begins E5 = D5, E6, E7, E8, E9 = \hat E8, etc."; the JACO paper (p. 181) likewise defines E_n as the extended series for all n >= 5 with E9 = affine E8. Nothing in the JAMS paper restricts to finite Weyl groups: the only finiteness used is finiteness of W_c (Lemma 4.3.1, p. 145), which Fan proves for the whole E series in Lemma 8.1.4 (p. 163), and the appendix lemmas 8.1.1-8.1.6 that drive the type-E step of Theorem 6.1.2 are stated and proved for arbitrary rank N+1. The base ring is Q(q^{1/2}) with the monomial basis b_w (b_s^2 = q2 b_s, b_s b_t b_s = b_s), q2 = q^{1/2}+q^{-1/2} = Green's delta; structure constants are powers of q2 (p. 141, Prop 5.4.1), so the cells are the same over Green's A = Z[v,v^{-1}], and Fan notes (p. 151) that the cell structure is independent of q. The cells of Fan Section 4.1 (p. 143) are exactly Green's Definition 4.3 monomial cells, and Fan states explicitly that they differ in general from Kazhdan-Lusztig cells, consistent with the manuscript's remark in the proof of Lemma maxI.

Every attribution in Green's Proposition 4.4 that Lemma maxI needs checks out against Fan: Green Def 4.1 = Fan Def 2.3.1 (p. 141; "i(S) is a substring of some reduced expression for w", with the heap-antichain reformulation on p. 142); Green 4.4(iv) = Fan Prop 4.4.3 (p. 148: "Let S in P. Let R = {w in W_c | w = i(S)x, a(w) = #S}. Then R is a right cell."); the left-hand version is licensed by Fan's explicit duality sentence on p. 148 and is itself used by Fan in the proof of Theorem 6.1.2 ("By proposition 4.4.3 and its dual, L(i(S)) = {v^{-1} | v in R(i(S))}"); and Green 4.4(v) is asserted by Fan himself in the introduction (p. 140: "each left and right cell in the same two-sided cell intersect in a single element (see proof of theorem 6.1.2)") and is indeed what the proof on p. 154 establishes, first for L(i(S)) ∩ R(i(S)) = {i(S)} by the induction and the Lemma 8.1.2 / Corollary 8.1.6 argument, and for arbitrary left/right cell pairs via the one-sided-multiplication bijections between cells (the map F in that proof and its dual), together with "#T(w) = #R(i(S))^2". The specific instance Lemma maxI uses is precisely the case Fan proves directly. So the structural input to Theorems uniform and affine and to the E10 proposition is sound for every rank, and the attribution chain manuscript -> Green -> Fan is accurate.

As an independent check I implemented the Temperley-Lieb monomial multiplication from Fan's defining relations (no cell theory assumed), enumerated all FC elements of E6-E11 (662, 2670, 10846, 44199, 180438, 737762, matching Fan's table on p. 156), computed the monomial left/right/two-sided cells as strongly connected components, and verified: the heap-width a-function agrees with the exponent in b_{w^{-1}} b_w = delta^{a(w)} b_d with d an involution (Fan Lemma 5.2.6); a is constant on cells; Prop 4.4.3 and its dual hold for every independent set (22, 36, 58, 94, 152, 246 sets); every left cell meets every right cell of the same two-sided cell in exactly one element; the number of two-sided cells equals |P/~| and the left-cell sizes reproduce Fan's irreducible-dimension table exactly in every rank; Green 4.4(iii) holds on all single steps; and the exact Lemma maxI instance (an FC x whose common descent set contains an independent set of size alpha(supp x) equals i(L(x) ∩ R(x))) has no counterexample in E6-E11, extending the critic's E9/E10/E13 brute-force checks.

The only substantive issue I found is one of credit rather than correctness: the abstract and introduction (lines 31, 114-115, 128-129) call the cell input "Green's monomial cell theory", "Green's monomial Temperley-Lieb cell theorem" and "Green's cell theory in generalized type E", whereas the theorem actually used (singleton intersection of monomial cells, and Prop 4.4.3) is Fan's; Green's Section 4 says so itself ("Most of this material comes from the papers [3] and [10]"). The manuscript credits Fan once, at line 213, which is good but should be reflected in the introductory wording. Minor notes: Fan's printed Prop 4.4.3 omits the word "reduced" (Green's gloss supplies the only meaningful reading); Green's proof of 4.4(iii) invokes Fan Lemma 4.2.5 without its non-cancellability hypothesis (repairable by Fan's "shifting" on p. 153, and not used by Lemma maxI); and since the bibliography already warns that Green's numbering is that of the 2007 preprint, adding Fan's own locators (Prop 4.4.3, p. 148; p. 140 and proof of Thm 6.1.2, p. 154) would make the citation robust. I consulted the repository's prior reports only at the end: research/journal-review/uniform-priority-followup.txt states that direct AMS retrieval of Fan failed there and that the Fan numbering was taken from Green, so this is the first check of the primary source; nothing in those reports changed my conclusions.

    CORRECTED: The abstract (line 31) and introduction (lines 114-115, 128-129) attribute the monomial cell theory to Green, whereas the two results actually used in Lemma maxI (right cell of i(S) = {w = i(S)x reduced, a(w) = #S}; left and right cells in one two-sided cell meet in a single element) are Fan's (JAMS 1997, Prop. 4.4.3 p. 148; p. 140 and proof of Thm. 6.1.2 p. 154), proved by Fan for the full infinite series E_n, and Green explicitly cites them as Fan's. VERIFIED_OK:
  + Fan's standing hypotheses cover the whole E series: p. 139, Section 1, 'we shall assume that Gamma is of type A, D, or E, where by E, we mean the infinite series E_n which begins E5 = D5, E6, E7, E8, E9 = \hat E8, etc.' Fan JACO 1996 p. 181 likewise: 'By E_n we mean the extended E series which is defined for n >= 5 ... Note that E9 = \hat E8.' Nothing restricts to finite or affine type; the only finiteness used is finiteness of W_c (Lemma 4.3.1 p. 145), proved for all E_n in Lemma 8.1.4 p. 163 and JACO Prop 2.
  + Base ring and basis: Fan works over Q(q^{1/2}) with the monomial basis b_w (p. 141, Sec 2.2), q2 = q^{1/2}+q^{-1/2} = Green's delta; structure constants are nonnegative powers of q2 (p. 141 and Prop 5.4.1 p. 150-151), so nonvanishing, hence the cells, is the same over Z[v,v^{-1}] (Green's A) and over Fan's field; Fan p. 151: 'the cell structure is independent of the parameter q.'
  + Fan's cells (Sec 4.1, p. 143) are defined by b_x b_w = c b_{w'} etc., identical to Green's Definition 4.3; Fan: 'In general, these cells do not agree with the Kazhdan-Lusztig cells, although they do in type A', consistent with the manuscript's sentence 'No identification of these monomial cells with ordinary KL cells is used' (line 232).
  + Green Definition 4.1 = Fan Definition 2.3.1 (p. 141): a(w) = max #S with i(S) a substring of some reduced expression; equivalently (p. 142) the maximal antichain size of the heap. The manuscript's description 'maximum size of a commuting product occurring as a length-additive factor' (lines 223-225) is equivalent. Checked computationally against Fan Lemma 5.2.6 (b_{w^{-1}} b_w = delta^{a(w)} b_d, d involution) for all FC elements of E6-E11: 0 failures.
  + Green 4.4(iv) = Fan Prop 4.4.3 (p. 148) verbatim up to the word 'reduced'; proof from Lemma 4.2.3 and Prop 4.4.2, all stated for the general series. Verified computationally for every independent set in E6-E11 (22, 36, 58, 94, 152, 246 sets): right cell of i(S) = {w : S subset L(w), a(w)=|S|}.
  + Left-hand version of 4.4(iv) used in Lemma maxI: Fan p. 148, 'Because of the symmetry of the defining relations, each statement we have made about right cancellability or right cells has a dual counterpart for left cancellability and left cells', and Fan uses 'proposition 4.4.3 and its dual' in the proof of Theorem 6.1.2 (p. 154). Verified computationally in E6-E11 (check C5).
  + Green 4.4(v): Fan asserts it in his introduction (p. 140) with pointer to the proof of Theorem 6.1.2; the proof (p. 154) shows L(i(S)) ∩ R(i(S)) = {i(S)} and '#left cells in T(w) = #R(i(S))', with '#T(w) = #R(i(S))^2', and the right-multiplication bijection F (plus its dual) transfers the singleton property to every left/right pair in the two-sided cell. The type-E step uses Lemma 8.1.2 and Corollary 8.1.6, both for arbitrary rank N+1 (Lemma 8.1.1 is quoted from Fan JACO 1996 Lemma 4, stated for E_n, n > 8, arbitrary n). Verified computationally in E6-E11: every left cell meets every right cel
  + The manuscript's claim (lines 214-216) that Lemma maxI makes explicit the final paragraph of Green's proof of Lemma 5.2: confirmed, research/en_literature/green-en-trace.txt lines 493-501 contain exactly the argument A subset L(z), z = i(A)z' reduced, 4.4(iv) gives z ~_R i(A), symmetric argument gives z ~_L i(A), 4.4(v) gives z = i(A).
  + Fan's irreducible-dimension table (p. 156) for ranks 6-11 is reproduced exactly by the computed left-cell sizes, and the number of two-sided cells equals |P/~| (Fan Thm 4.5.1) in each rank; FC counts 662, 2670, 10846, 44199, 180438, 737762 match Fan's table.
  + Exact Lemma maxI instance checked by brute force in E6-E11: every FC x whose common descent set L(x) ∩ R(x) contains an independent set of size alpha(supp x) equals i(L(x) ∩ R(x)); 0 violations (22, 36, 58, 94, 152, 246 such elements, all commuting products).
  + Lemma maxI is applied only inside E_n groups (lines 246, 319, 563, 669 and Appendix E10); the induced support graphs D4, D4+A1, D6, E7, E8 at line 316 are subgraphs of the ambient E_n, so Fan's hypotheses apply. Fan's warning that Lemma 4.3.2 fails in affine D4 (p. 147) is not relevant to any use in the manuscript.
  + Bibliographic data for Fan (line 949-951): title, journal, volume 10, year 1997, pages 139-167, DOI S0894-0347-97-00222-1 all match the retrieved PDF header.
  + Fan's a-function and the Lusztig a-function: Fan p. 141-142 says equality is known for finite W and conjectured in general; Green Remark 4.5 cites Shi for finite and affine Weyl groups. The manuscript does not rely on this identification.

UNRESOLVED:

Fan's proof of Theorem 6.1.2 is terse (e.g. the map F on p. 154 appears to be written with b_v where b_x is meant, and the step 'we deduce that i(S) = N' from Corollary 8.1.6 is left to the reader); I checked its logic by hand for arbitrary rank and computationally for n <= 11, but did not produce a fully formalised re-proof for all n.
  ? The published JKTR 18 (2009) version of Green's paper was not retrieved; proposition numbering was checked only against the 2007 arXiv preprint already archived in the repository.

==================== followup-uniform-all-k ====================
TASK: Upgrade Theorem uniform (lines 96-108) from 'vanish for all sufficiently large k' to 'mu(x,b_{r,k}) = 0 for every FC x and every k >= 0', at least for r = 3 and ideally for all r. Ingredients already established in my scratchpad (/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-ic-workspace-kl/45cb4eb2-517e-46a6-a21d-77de64370e52/scratchpad/completeness/check_uniform_covers.py, fc_en2.cpp): maximum FC length in E11, E12, E13 is 66, 78, 92; l(b_{r,k}) = 8r^2+3+116k for the six tested (r,k); none of the Bruhat covers of b_{3,0}, b_{3,1}, b_{3,2}, b_{4,0}, b_{4,1}, b_{5,0} is FC. Remaining work: (a) prove the length formula 8r^2+3+116k (e.g., by the affine inversion-string method of Theorem affine applied in the E8-affine parabolic, or by Lemma rootaction and a depth formula); (b) prove a bound on the maximum FC length of E_{4r+1} below 8r^2+2 for all r >= 3 (the data 16,27,29,44,55,66,78,92 for n=6..13 suggest roughly linear growth, so a heap/Stembridge-type argument should give O(n) or at worst a polynomial bound), or else record the computed E13 maximum and the k=0 cover certificate as a theorem for r=3; (c) state the effective threshold.
SUMMARY:
The investigation asked whether Theorem uniform (results/exceptional-leading.tex, lines 96-108) can be upgraded from "vanish for all sufficiently large k" to "mu(x, b_{r,k}) = 0 for every FC x and every k >= 0", via (a) the length formula l(b_{r,k}) = 8r^2+3+116k, (b) an upper bound on the maximum FC length of E_{4r+1} below 8r^2+2, and (c) an effective threshold. I reproduced all of the completeness critic's numerical inputs with fresh code (length formula on 29 (r,k) pairs including r up to 10; no FC Bruhat covers for (r,k) in {(3,0),(3,1),(3,2),(4,0),(4,1),(5,0),(6,0),(7,0)}; FC counts and maximum lengths for E6-E15, agreeing with the critic for E11-E13 and extending to E14 (50,864,885 elements, max 106) and E15 (209,095,841 elements, max 121)). Route (b), however, is provably impossible: an explicit FC family (width-2 staircase blocks separated by the branch leaf), verified reduced and FC for all odd n up to 101, has length 8r^2+7r-2 in E_{4r+1}, which exceeds l(b_{r,0})-1 = 8r^2+2 for every r and exceeds l(b_{r,k})-1 whenever 7r-3 > 116k. The maximum FC length of E_n grows like (n-1)^2/2 + Theta(n), not linearly as the critic read the data, so no length-based argument can ever handle k = 0, and for fixed k >= 1 it fails from r >= 18 on.

The good news is that the strong statement is nevertheless true for all r >= 3 and all k >= 0, with a short uniform proof that needs neither the length formula nor any FC-length bound. By Deodhar's lifting property, every Bruhat cover x of b = b_{r,k} other than the 2(2r+1) elements sb, bs (s in I_r = L(b) = R(b)) satisfies L(x), R(x) ⊇ I_r, so if FC it equals i(I_r) by Lemma maxI, whose length 2r+1 is odd while l(b)-1 is even. For x = bs one has s in L(x) (because b alpha_s = alpha_s - m_s beta is not ±alpha_s), and s x = s b s = r_{s beta} is a reflection whose root s beta = beta - m_s alpha_s has full support minus at most one node, hence is non-simple; in a simply laced Coxeter group a non-simple reflection is never fully commutative (its reduced word w^{-1} s w, with w of minimal length taking beta to a simple root, contains the braid factor t s t; this uses l(r_beta) = 2 dp(beta) - 1, a standard fact going back to Brink-Howlett and stated e.g. in Petersen-Tenner). Since FC is inherited by weak-order prefixes, bs is not FC; sb = (bs)^{-1} likewise. All ingredients were also verified numerically (bs has L = I_r, R = I_r \ {s} and is non-FC for r = 3..12 and many k; sbs = r_{s beta} with l(sbs) = l(bs)-1; no FC reflection of length >= 3 among all positive roots of E6, E7, E8). This also strengthens Proposition thm:indefinite (E10 reflections) to full vanishing, and it reduces the affine cover exclusion in Theorem affine to checking 10 explicit elements (there b_0 is an involution with a 3-dimensional (-1)-eigenspace, not a reflection, so the computational check remains necessary but can be made much lighter than the 44,199-element FC catalogue).

On the length formula: the slope 116 = 2 l(T) is now proved for all r, k. T = r_gamma r_{gamma+delta} is the translation by gamma^vee in the affine E8 parabolic, l(T^k) = 58k, and l(T^k b T^{-k}) = l(b) + 116k holds iff (alpha, beta_r) <= -1 for every alpha in N(T^k) = {eta + m delta : (eta,gamma) >= 1, eps(eta) <= m < k(eta,gamma)+eps(eta)}; since (eta, beta_r) = (r-2)(eta_0 - eta_1) + f(eta) is affine-linear in r, this is a finite check on the 120 positive E8 roots (coefficient signs plus the value at r = 3), which passes with zero violations. The constant l(b_{r,0}) = 8r^2+3 has a proved upper bound (the depth of beta_r is at most 4r^2+1 via the minimal coset representative of the even sign change F in W(D_{4r})/S_{4r}, so l <= 8r^2+3) and is verified exactly for r = 3..12, but I did not find a uniform proof of the matching lower bound; it is not needed for the upgraded theorem.

Recommendation for the authors: replace the last clause of Theorem uniform and the corresponding sentences in the abstract (line 33) and at lines 578-580 and 589-590 by the unconditional statement mu(x, b_{r,k}) = 0 for all FC x, with the lifting/reflection argument (about ten lines); record the FC-length data (E13: 92, E14: 106, E15: 121; explicit family of length 8r^2+7r-2) to explain why a length-based threshold cannot work; and, if the length formula is kept, present the slope as proved and the constant as computationally certified or supply the missing lower bound.

--- [major|confirmed|conf=high] followup-uniform-all-k-D2: Theorem uniform can be upgraded to mu(x,b_{r,k}) = 0 for all FC x, all r >= 3, all k >= 0, with a short uniform proof (lifting property + non-FC reflections); the 'no effective threshold' sentence should go
  LOC: results/exceptional-leading.tex lines 96-108 (Theorem uniform), 33 (abstract), 554-581 (proof), 589-590
  CLAIM: The weak clause 'for each fixed r, all these leading coefficients vanish for every sufficiently large k' and the sentence 'The argument does not give an effective threshold' undersell a result that is provable in full strength without any length formula or FC bound: every Bruhat cover of b_{r,k} fails to be fully commutative. Proof: (1) by the lifting property, for s in L(b)=I_r either s in L(x) or x = sb (and symmetrically on the right); a cover x not in {sb, bs} has L(x),R(x) ⊇ I_r and, if FC, equals i(I_r) by Lemma maxI, impossible since l(i(I_r)) = 2r+1 is odd and l(b)-1 is even (b is a reflection). (2) For x = bs: s in L(x) because bs = sb would force b alpha_s = ±alpha_s, while b alpha_s = alpha_s - m_s beta with m_s >= 1; then sx = sbs = r_{s beta} with s beta = beta - m_s alpha_s non-simple (all other coordinates of beta are >= 1 and n >= 13). A non-simple reflection in a simply laced group is never FC: with w of minimal length such that w beta' = alpha_t, the word w^{-1} t w is reduced (l(r_beta') = 2dp(beta')-1) and contains the factor u t u with u the last letter of w, which must not commute with t, hence is a braid. FC is inherited by left-weak-order prefixes, so x = bs is not FC; sb = (bs)^{-1} likewise.

==================== followup-e6-e7-global-mu ====================
TASK: Determine whether W(E6) (51,840 elements) contains any pair x < w with mu(x,w) >= 2 for arbitrary (not necessarily FC) x, and do the same for W(E7) if feasible. Method: run du Cloux's Coxeter3 (or Geck's PyCox 'klpolynomials' for E6, or a C++ KL implementation on the full group) to produce all mu-values, or locate a published statement (Alvis's E6 computations, Geck-Halls, FindStat St001144, du Cloux's 1996 AAECC paper) giving the maximum mu in E6/E7. Record the maximum mu and, if it exceeds 1, an explicit pair and whether its bottom is FC.
SUMMARY:
Question investigated: does W(E6) (and W(E7)) contain pairs x < w with mu(x,w) >= 2 when x is NOT required to be fully commutative? Answer: yes, emphatically. By a fresh, exact-integer C++ computation of all Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials of W(E6) (51,840 elements, 466,250,713 Bruhat pairs, 6,579,176 extremal pairs, 46,681 distinct polynomials, maximal coefficient 748) the maximum unrestricted mu-value in E6 is 10, attained by 8 pairs; in total 1,386 pairs have mu >= 2 (400 with mu=2, 556 with mu=3, 310 with mu=4, 108 with mu=5, 4 with mu=6, 8 with mu=10). An explicit witness in the manuscript's numbering (chain 0-1-2-3-4, node 5 on node 2) is x = 01254012010 (length 11, not FC, L(x)=R(x)={0,1,2,4,5}), w = 123012523412301251234012 (length 24, full support), P_{x,w} = 1+13q+59q^2+121q^3+125q^4+61q^5+10q^6. Every lower endpoint of every pair with mu >= 2 is non-FC; among the 6,431 pairs in E6 with FC lower endpoint and nonzero mu, every mu equals 1, which is an exhaustive brute-force confirmation of Theorem main in type E6 independent of the authors' star-reduction argument.

For E7 a full table was not feasible here (my run reached 223 million extremal pairs by length 24 and was stopped for memory), but the existence question is settled: W(E6) is a standard parabolic subgroup of W(E7) (nodes {0,1,2,3,4,6}), and W(D6) another (nodes {1,...,6}), and KL polynomials of a standard parabolic agree with the ambient group's. Hence max mu in E7 >= 10 and in E8 >= 10 (via E7). I verified this concretely by recomputing the embedded pair x = 01264012010, w = 123012623412301261234012 directly inside W(E7) with an independent R-polynomial method (same polynomial, mu = 10), and likewise a D6 pair with mu = 4 inside E7. D6 itself has max mu 4 (|W|=23,040; 46 pairs with mu >= 2), with FC-bottom maximum 1, consistent with Gern's type-D theorem.

Validation of the computation: (i) exact agreement of the complete KL tables with a separately written naive Python implementation (right-sided recursion, full storage) on A3, B3, D4, A4, A5, D5 (up to 745,377 pairs); (ii) every extremal pair in D6, E6, A6, A7 recomputed by the right-sided recursion and compared with the left-sided one; (iii) three individual pairs recomputed from the defining R-polynomial identity q^{l(w)-l(x)}P(q^{-1}) - P(q) = sum R_{x,y}P_{y,w}; (iv) agreement of all FindStat St001144 values I could reach (A1-A7, B3, B4, B5 = 2, D4, D5) and of FC counts with Stembridge's (662 for E6, 2670 for E7); (v) bitset and descent-bucket Bruhat modes give identical E6 results; (vi) positivity, constant term 1 and degree bounds asserted on every polynomial.

Consequence for the manuscript: the WHY of this follow-up had two branches, and the second one holds. The E6/E7/E8 theorem is not a corollary of an all-pairs 0/1 bound; the FC hypothesis on the lower endpoint is doing real work, since unrestricted mu reaches 10 already in E6 and in a D6 parabolic reaches 4. The manuscript currently says nothing about this (no sentence states that the FC hypothesis is necessary or quotes any unrestricted mu value), and the authors' own priority report leaves it open. Adding one sentence and one explicit pair would sharpen the significance statement and close the open point. A caveat worth stating in the paper: the E6 case (and, with Coxeter3, the E7 case) is nonetheless directly machine-checkable from a complete mu-table (my E6 run takes 2 seconds); the reduction argument is indispensable only for E8. I consulted the repository's prior AI reports only after forming these conclusions; they frame the question (significance.txt, finite.txt, exceptional_priority/report.txt lines 175-179) but none computed unrestricted mu in D6, E6 or E7.

VERIFIED_OK:
  + Theorem main in type E6 (mu(x,w) in {0,1} for every FC x and every w) holds by exhaustive computation: all 6,579,176 extremal pairs of W(E6) computed; among 6,431 pairs with FC lower endpoint and nonzero mu, the maximum is 1 (e6.out).
  + Gern's type-D theorem in D4, D5, D6 and Green's type-A theorem in A3-A7: in each group the maximum mu over pairs with FC lower endpoint is 1 (klmu runs), while unrestricted mu reaches 4 in D6.
  + FC element counts used in the manuscript/certificates: 662 for E6 and 2670 for E7 reproduced (also Catalan counts for A_n and Stembridge's 48/167/593 for D4/D5/D6).
  + KL polynomials of a standard parabolic subgroup agree with the ambient group (used by the manuscript for the D6 pair in E7/E8): confirmed concretely by recomputing a D6 pair (mu=4) and an E6 pair (mu=10) directly inside W(E7) via R-polynomials (rp_e7_d6pair.txt, rp_e7_e6pair.txt).
  + Correctness of my own tool: full KL tables identical to an independent naive Python implementation on A3 (213 pairs), B3 (847), D4 (9,817), A4 (3,781), A5 (98,407), D5 (745,377); left- and right-sided recursions agree on every extremal pair of D6, E6, A6, A7; three individual polynomials reproduced from the R-polynomial identity; all FindStat St001144 values reachable here (A1-A7, B3-B5, D4, D5) reproduced, including the positive control B5 = 2 (exactly 4 pairs).
  + The manuscript's statement (lines 365-368) that the finite proof needs no large KL table is accurate as a description of the proof; the E6 star-reduction route and the brute-force route give the same conclusion.

UNRESOLVED:
  ? Exact maximum of mu in W(E7) and W(E8): only the lower bound 10 (from the E6 parabolic) is established. My full E7 computation reached 222,956,843 extremal pairs by length 24 (of 63) in 105 s and was stopped when the machine's free memory fell to ~140 MB; a complete E7 table needs Coxeter3-scale memory management and was not feasible in this session.
  ? Published cross-check of my E6 statistics (6,579,176 extremal pairs, 46,681 distinct polynomials, maximal coefficient 748, max mu 10): du Cloux's 2002 Experimental Mathematics paper could not be fetched (EMIS 503, HAL access denied, emis.de redirect), and no other source stating the maximal mu in E6 was found; I am not aware of the value 10 appearing in print.
  ? Whether the mu = 5 FC-upper-endpoint phenomenon (finding D3) is already noted in the literature was not determined.

==================== followup-e9-odd-gap-pairs ====================
TASK: Compute the unresolved leading coefficients for the authors' second affine E8 reflection family described in research/en_families/report.txt lines 95-109 and research/en-search-status.txt lines 41-47: w = r_beta with beta = (1,2,3,3,2,2,1,1,2) in the paper's E9 coordinates, length 33, L(w)=R(w)={3,5,7,8} (size 4, not a maximum independent set, so Lemma maxI does not apply), with five eligible FC bottoms of lengths 4, 5, 5, 6, 6 (odd gaps 29, 27, 27 for the length-4 and length-6 bottoms), and its two right extensions w s0, w s1 of length 34 with a unique eligible FC bottom of length 5 (gap 29); lengths 33+58k and 34+58k for the translated versions. Compute P_{x,w} for these pairs with a C++ KL implementation over the lower Bruhat ideal (the authors' run hit a 3-million-state cap; the length-27 ideal has 740,128 elements, so several million states with compact storage is realistic), or bound mu via Lusztig's a-function as Gern does in thesis Chapter 4 (research/en_families reports failed parabolic-factor screens, so the ideal computation is the direct route). Report mu for each pair.
SUMMARY:
The task was to determine the leading coefficients that the authors left "UNRESOLVED" for the second affine-E8 reflection family: w = r_beta with beta=(1,2,3,3,2,2,1,1,2) (length 33, L=R={3,5,7,8}, five eligible FC bottoms of lengths 4,5,5,6,6) and its right extensions w s0, w s1 (length 34, unique eligible FC bottom of length 5). I wrote an independent C++ KL engine (scratchpad/followup-e9-odd-gap-pairs/klq.cpp) that runs the KL recursion in the parabolic quotient H C'_{w_I} (maximal right-coset representatives for I = R(w)), which shrinks the 5.8-million-element lower ideal to 364,156 cosets and lets a lazily memoised du Cloux-style recursion finish in about 5 seconds and 200 MB. The algorithm was validated against (i) my own naive full-ideal KL code on 900 random (w,I) pairs in A4, D4, E6 and affine E8, (ii) the authors' separate Python implementation targeted.py on 4,172 pairs, (iii) the known D4 value P_{x,xcx}=1+2q, and (iv) the inversion symmetry P(x,w)=P(x^{-1},w) on all 27,311 extremal pairs of the length-33 table (never used by the algorithm); randomised recursion paths gave identical polynomials.

Results (all exact integers): mu(8753, w)=0 (gap 29; P = 1+23q+...+346q^12+23q^13 has degree 13 < 14); mu(87531,w)=mu(87530,w)=0 (even gaps 28); mu(875301,w)=mu(875310,w)=1 (gap 27; P = 1+23q+234q^2+...+40q^12+q^13); mu(87530, w s0)=1 and mu(87531, w s1)=1 (gap 29; P = 1+18q+...+10q^13+q^14). Hence every coefficient that parity left open for k=0 lies in {0,1}, which supports the conjectural FC-bottom bound in affine E8 and gives no analogue of the affine-D4 failure in results/star-boundary.tex. As a by-product the authors' a-function claim mu=0 for the length-32 terminal is confirmed directly (deg P = 12 < 13), and the length-27 ideal size 740,128 and its unique eligible bottom are reproduced exactly.

Two consequences for the manuscript. First, the values should be reported: the status documents still say UNRESOLVED, and Section 5 presents a vanishing family while its immediate neighbours (same root up to delta, same descent-set mechanism) have genuinely nonzero odd-gap coefficients. Second, the extensions w s0, w s1 are explicit witnesses that the parity hypothesis in Proposition prop:parity cannot be dropped: they have L=R=I a maximum independent set of size 5, length 34 of the wrong parity, and mu(i(I),b)=1 at gap 29. The remark in research/en_families/report.txt that the KL computation is "otherwise expensive" is not accurate: with a one-sided parabolic quotient it is trivial. The translated elements r_{beta+k delta} (lengths 91, 149, ...) remain out of reach by direct computation (the number of affine-E8 elements of length <= 91 is about 4.2x10^11), and the k=0 data show a(w)<=7 and a(w s_i)<=6, so Lusztig's a-function degree bound cannot settle them either; for k>=1 the question stays open, but nothing in the manuscript depends on it.

FINDINGS:

==================== followup-d8-mu-recompute ====================
TASK: Independently recompute mu(x8,w8) in W(D8), where x8 = s1 s2 s4 s6 s8 and w8 is Gern's bad element (thesis Theorem 2.2.18 with m = 8, signed permutation (1,-8,3,-6,5,-4,7,-2) under Gern's conventions; lengths 5 and 26), using an exact-integer KL implementation on the lower ideal of w8 (265,760 elements) in two independent group models (signed permutations with Gern's Example 1.1.6 conventions, and the geometric representation). Report the full polynomial P_{x8,w8}, the coefficient of q^10, and P_{e,w8} as a check of Gern Lemma 2.3.9.
SUMMARY:
I independently recomputed the one computer-derived input to results/kl-transfer.tex that nobody in the repository had reproduced: mu(x8,w8) in W(D8), where w8 = (1,-8,3,-6,5,-4,7,-2) is Gern's bad element (thesis Theorem 2.2.18 / Corollary 2.2.19, m=8) and x8 = s1 s2 s4 s6 s8. Fresh C++ code (exact 64-bit integer arithmetic, extremal-pair KL recurrence from KL79 (2.2.c) transported to right descents) was run on the Bruhat lower ideal of w8, which has exactly 265,760 elements, in two independent group models: Gern's signed-permutation model (Example 1.1.6 conventions: s1 acts on the right by (w1,w2) -> (-w2,-w1), s_i (i>=2) swaps positions i-1,i; length = inversions + pairs with w_i + w_j < 0; descents by Prop 2.2.4) and the geometric representation on the root lattice of the Gern-labelled D8 diagram (s1-s3, s2-s3, s3-...-s8; 56 positive roots; length = number of positive roots sent negative; descents by sign of w(alpha_i)). Both models, and two different descent-selection policies, give the identical full vector P_{.,w8} over all 265,760 elements (compared via canonical reduced words).

Result: P_{x8,w8} = P_{e,w8} = 1 + 12q + 59q^2 + 154q^3 + 233q^4 + 221q^5 + 147q^6 + 70q^7 + 20q^8 + 2q^9. The degree is exactly 9, so the coefficient of q^10 (= mu(x8,w8), since (26-5-1)/2 = 10) is 0. Gern's Lemma 4.5.5 (thesis line 3439) is therefore confirmed, and the n=8 row of the kl-transfer table (L=26, l(x)=5, rank 21, degree bound 9, mu=0; kl-transfer.tex line 232) is correct; indeed the "improved" bound deg <= 9 of Theorem thm:bad is sharp. P_{e,w8} was computed by the direct recurrence at (e,w8) without the P_{x,w}=P_{xs,w} shortcut and equals P_{x8,w8}, confirming Lemma 2.3.9 at n=8. Lusztig's bound deg P_{e,w8} <= (26 - a(w8))/2 = 10 with a(w8)=6 (Prop 4.5.1) is consistent with the computed degree 9. The interval [x8,w8] has 163,724 elements with rank vector (1,16,108,431,1210,2668,4952,8074,11857,15873,19441,21741,22030,19934,15743,10523,5736,2439,764,162,20,1).

Engine validation independent of the D8 target: (i) D6 reproduces the repository's P_{x6,w6} = 1+6q+11q^2+6q^3+q^4+q^5, lower ideal 3,184, interval 1,676 with rank vector (1,12,55,140,248,339,360,287,162,59,12,1); (ii) a separately written Python brute force (Gern's Prop 1.2.6 with LEFT descents, subword Bruhat test, dict polynomials) over the full groups D4 (192 elements, 9,817 comparable pairs, matching the repository's own D4 count) and D5 (1,920 elements, 745,377 comparable pairs) agrees with every one of the 608 and 21,776 extremal pairs stored by the C++ engine, with zero mismatches; (iii) all 3,533 distinct polynomials in the D8 table have constant term 1 and nonnegative coefficients, and for every x in the ideal the recurrence at w8 gives the same answer for all five right descents of w8. I also verified that Gern's Lemma 2.3.4 reduced word for w8, [2,0][4,0][6,0][8,0][6,4][7,6][8,8] = s2 s1 s4 s3 s2 s1 s6 s5 s4 s3 s2 s1 s8 s7 s6 s5 s4 s3 s2 s1 s6 s5 s4 s7 s6 s8, evaluates to (1,-8,3,-6,5,-4,7,-2) with length 26.

No error was found in this dimension. The findings below are a note recommending that the manuscripts replace the "inherited from the thesis, not independently reproduced" caveat with the actual polynomial (the computation takes under ten seconds on this machine), and a minor remark about an off-by-one typo in the thesis's Proposition 2.2.3 that the repository's conventions correctly do not follow. I consulted the existing AI referee reports only after finishing; none of them recompute or discuss the D8 value, so nothing there changed my assessment.

FINDINGS:

--- [minor|unverified-minor|conf=high] followup-d8-mu-recompute-D2: Off-by-one in thesis Proposition 2.2.3 as printed; repository conventions correctly follow Example 1.1.6
  LOC: sources/gern-thesis-2013.txt lines 749-755 (Proposition 2.2.3) versus lines 185-191 (Example 1.1.6) and lines 764-769 (Proposition 2.2.4); computations/README.md lines 17-22
  CLAIM: The thesis's Proposition 2.2.3 says right multiplication by s_i (i >= 2) interchanges w(i) and w(i+1), but the embedding in Example 1.1.6 (s_i -> (i-1,i)(-(i-1),-i)), the descent formula of Proposition 2.2.4 (w(i-1) > w(i)) and Example 2.2.1 all require s_i to swap positions i-1 and i. This is a typo in the external source, not in the repository, but readers reconciling the repository's conventions with the thesis could be misled.
  EVIDENCE: conv.py: under the Example 1.1.6 convention s2 s3 s1 s2 s4 in D4 evaluates to (-2,-3,4,1), matching Example 2.2.1 (bars lost in text extraction); under the literal Proposition 2.2.3 convention s4 would act on positions 4,5, which do not exist in D4. The repository README states 'generator i>0 swaps the entries at one-based positions i,i+1 ... code labels 0,1,2,... correspond to Gern's s_1,s_2,s_3', i.e. Gern's s_{i+1} swaps positions i,i+1, which is the Example 1.1.6 convention.
  FIX: Optionally add one sentence to computations/README.md noting that the conventions follow Gern's Example 1.1.6 and Proposition 2.2.4, and that Proposition 2.2.3 as printed has an index shift.

  + mu(x8,w8) = 0 (Gern Lemma 4.5.5, thesis line 3439; kl-transfer.tex eq:mu and table row n=8): the coefficient of q^10 in P_{x8,w8} is 0 because deg P_{x8,w8} = 9. Verified in two independent group models and two descent policies.
  + Lemma 2.3.9 at n=8: P_{e,w8} computed by the direct KL recurrence at the pair (e,w8), without using P_{x,w}=P_{xs,w}, equals P_{x8,w8}.

==================== followup-new-terminals-structure ====================
TASK: Give a structural description of the two genuinely new exceptional noncommuting terminals, the E7 element 1325436210321432543621324356 (length 28, L=R={1,3,5,6}) and the E8 element b_{8,6} (length 50, L=R={1,3,5,7}), and add the D-parabolic consistency statement to the paper. Concretely: (a) verify computationally the hand check that every other row of Table 1 is a Gern type-D bad element (w4, w4 u, w6 of Theorem 2.3.6) under the label map 1,2,...,7 -> 1,n-1,2,3,4,5,6 into the D7 (resp. D6, D5) parabolic, so that the table is 'Gern's list plus two'; (b) compute for the two new elements the (-1)-eigenspace dimension, the root-inversion set, whether each is the longest element of a parabolic double coset or a product of commuting reflections (as w6 is a product of two reflections in D6), and whether the E8 element arises from the E7 element by the same 'doubling' pattern that produces w6 from w4 (compare Gern Lemma 2.3.4's bracket form); (c) check whether either element is maximal-length among terminals of its type and whether either admits a length-additive parabolic factorization that would give an a-function bound independent of parity.
SUMMARY:
I rebuilt the E_n reflection representation, Gern's D_m conventions (fork leaves 1,2 on 3; s_1=(1,-2)(-1,2), s_i=(i-1,i)) and his bracket form (Lemma 2.3.4) from scratch in /private/tmp/.../scratchpad/followup-new-terminals-structure/, and checked the thesis against itself first: for n=4..10 the bracket word reproduces the signed permutations of Corollary 2.2.19 and has length 3n^2/8+n/4. Under the paper's label map (Gern 1->1, 2->n-1, j->j-1) the elements w4, w4*u, w6 land exactly (matrix equality) on the nine non-new rows of Table 1: E6 row = w4; E7 rows = w4, w4*s6, w6; E8 rows = w4, w4*s6, w4*s7, w6. An exhaustive signed-permutation enumeration of D7 (322,560 elements) independently finds exactly the bad elements {w4, w4 s6, w4 s7, w6}, with 3 of them in D6 and 1 in D5, i.e. Gern's Theorem 2.3.6 in ranks 5-7, and the E8 length-28 row is literally the E7 new element with 6 relabelled 7. So Table 1 is "Gern's list plus two", and the paper never says so (Proposition/Table at lines 277-304 and the proof at 386-395 only identify the length-15 rows). An earlier draft (research/editorial/source-before-polish.txt:313-315) even had an inheritance sentence that was dropped in polishing. This should be restored and strengthened; it is both an attribution point and a cheap exhaustiveness cross-check.

Independently of the repository's engines I re-derived the finite classification: brute force BFS over all of E6 (51,840) and E7 (2,903,040) and a one-step parabolic pruning in E8 (240 minimal coset representatives x my 576 E7 right-terminals = 138,240 candidates) give 72/23/1, 576/40/4 and 2160/64/6 (right-terminal / two-sided / noncommuting), the same six E8 words, and the length-28 and length-50 elements are the unique longest noncommuting terminals in E7 and E8. The E_n analogue of Gern's Theorem 2.3.6 holds verbatim in ranks 6-8: the noncommuting terminals are b_k * u with b_4=w4, b_6=w6, b_7 (length 28), b_8 (length 50), and u a commuting product of generators neither in nor adjacent to supp(b_k); the only such u are s5 (E7) and s5, s6 (E8), all on w4.

Structurally, both new elements are involutions but neither is a reflection: the E7 element is a product of four mutually orthogonal reflections (type 4A1, roots 0011111, 0111101, 0121001, 1222111 of heights 5,5,5,10; the four roots do not form a D4 frame) and the E8 element is a product of two orthogonal reflections (type 2A1, roots 12332212 and 13432102 of height 16, fixed subsystem D6). Both have the free-double-coset form b = i(I) d i(I) with l(d)=l(b)-8, and iterating this peeling yields a reduced palindrome of independent-set layers b = P i(K) P^{-1} whose layers alternate between the two colour classes of the bipartite E_n diagram: w4 = (13)(2)(5)(2)(13), w6 = (1356)(24)(136)(24)(1356), b_7 = (1356)(24)(136)(024)(1356)(024)(136)(24)(1356), b_8 = (1357)(24)(137)(024)(1357)(0246)(1357)(24)(1357)(0246)(1357)(024)(137)(24)(1357). The negated roots are exactly P(alpha_k), k in K, which explains the involution types (A1, 3A1, 4A1, 2A1). The four elements form a chain in both weak orders w4 <_R w6 <_R b_7 <_R b_8, as do Gern's own w4 <_R w6 <_R w8 <_R w10 in D10, but the "doubling" from w4 to w6 is not reproduced from b_7 to b_8 in any literal sense (length increments 8,13,22 versus Gern's 8,11,14; involution types 1,3,4,2 versus Gern's 1,3,2,4). Note that the critic's premise that w6 is a product of two reflections is wrong: w6=(-1,-6,3,-4,5,-2) negates e1, e4 and e2+e6, so it is 3A1.

Finally, both new coefficients can be shown to vanish without parity: each element has a length-additive factor w_0(J) with J = I u {0} of type A2 x A1^3, l(w_0(J)) = 6 = |I|+2, hence a(b) >= 6 by Lusztig's monotonicity, and with P_{i(I),b} = P_{e,b} (I subset of L(b)) and deg P_{e,b} <= (l(b)-a(b))/2 one gets mu(i(I),b)=0. As a consistency check the same method gives exactly 5 for Gern's w6, matching a(w6)=5 (Gern Prop. 4.5.1) and the nonzero mu(x6,w6)=1. The paper's parity argument is correct as written; the a-function remark would make the finite theorem's genuinely new content robust and is cheap to add. I consulted the repository's AI referee/editorial reports only at the end (grep for Gern 2.3.6, involution, a-function, palindrome, b_{8,6}); none of them contains the Gern-plus-two statement, the structural description or the a-function remark, so these are not duplicates.

--- [note|unverified-minor|conf=high] followup-new-terminals-structure-D3: No structural description of the two genuinely new terminals; they are palindromic layered involutions b = P i(K) P^{-1}
  LOC: results/exceptional-leading.tex lines 283-308 (Table 1 and the b_{8,6} word)
  CLAIM: The paper gives the two new elements only as 28- and 50-letter strings. They have a clean structure that also covers Gern's w4 and w6: each is an involution with L=R=I commuting, the double coset W_I b W_I is free (|W_I b W_I| = 256 = |W_I|^2, so b = i(I) d i(I) with l(d)=l(b)-8), and iterating this peeling writes b as a reduced palindrome of independent-set layers alternating between the two colour classes of the bipartite diagram, b = P i(K) P^{-1} with l(b)=2 l(P)+|K|. Consequently b is the product of the |K| mutually orthogonal reflections in P(alpha_k): E7 element = 4A1 (roots 0011111, 0111101, 0121001, 1222111, heights 5,5,5,10, not a D4 frame since their sum 1465324 is not in 2*Phi), E8 element = 2A1 (roots 12332212, 13432102, height 16, fixed subsystem D6). Gern's w4 is A1 (= r_{0111001}) and w6 is 3A1 (= r_{0011001} r_{0111000} r_{0111111} in E7 labels), not 'two reflections'. The four elements form a chain w4 <_R w6 <_R b_7 <_R b_8 in right (and, being involutions, left) weak order, as do Gern's w4 <_R w6 <_R w8 <_R w10 in D10; but the E7->E8 step is not Gern's w4->w6 'doubling' (length increments 8,13,22 vs 8,11,14; involution types 1,3,4,2 vs 1,3,2,4).
  EVIDENCE: part_b4_palindrome.py: w4 = (13)(2)(5)(2)(13) [E6]; w6 = (1356)(24)(136)(24)(1356); b_7 = (1356)(24)(136)(024)(1356)(024)(136)(24)(1356), l(P)=12, l=28; b_8 = (1357)(24)(137)(024)(1357)(0246)(1357)(24)(1357)(0246)(1357)(024)(137)(24)(1357), l(P)=24, l=50; in each case the concatenated word equals the table element and has length equal to the inversion count (hence reduced), the layers alternate colour classes, and {P(alpha_k)} equals the set of positive roots negated by b. part_b3_chains.py: peeling lengths 28->20->16->10->4 (E7) and 50->42->38->32->26->18->10->2 (E8) with descent sets alternating {1,3,5,n-1},{2,4},{1,3,n-1},{0,2,4},...; Gern chain w4<w6<w8<w10 prefix and suffix tests all True. part_b2_more_structure.py: w6 is a prefix and suffix of b_7 with quotient of length 13 (word 4230126345231); b_7 (relabelled) is a prefix of b_8 with quotient of length 22 (word 6543275341230127345231); d=i(I)b i(I) has L(d)=R(d)={2,4} in both cases.
  FIX: Add a short remark or proposition giving the layered palindromic form of all four noncommuting terminals b_4,b_6,b_7,b_8, their involution types, and the weak-order chain; this answers the conceptual-description request at essentially zero cost and makes the two new rows readable.

--- [note|unverified-minor|conf=high] followup-new-terminals-structure-D4: Both new coefficients also vanish by an a-function bound independent of parity (robustness remark missing)
  LOC: results/exceptional-leading.tex lines 382-397 (proof of Theorem thm:main: 'Table 1 and parity leave only the length-15 rows')
  CLAIM: The vanishing of mu(x,b) for the two new elements currently rests only on the even gaps 24 and 46. Each new element admits a length-additive factorization b = u w_0(J) v with J = I u {0} of type A2 x A1^3, l(w_0(J)) = 6 = |I|+2. Hence a(b) >= a(w_0(J)) = 6 (b <=_LR w_0(J), Lusztig P4 and P12 in finite Weyl groups), and since P_{i(I),b} = P_{e,b} (I subset of L(b)) with deg P_{e,b} <= (l(b)-a(b))/2 <= (l(b)-6)/2 < (l(b)-|I|-1)/2, the coefficient mu(i(I),b) vanishes with no parity input. No w_0(A3) factor exists, so 6 is the best bound obtainable this way; it is exactly enough.
  EVIDENCE: part_b2_more_structure.py: E7: u=31265234312, w_0(J)=653010, v=42312645231 (11+6+11=28, additive True); E8: u=31275234312, w_0(J)=753010, v=276453423127563452341230127345231 (11+6+33=50, additive True); maximum of l(w_0(L(V))) over all 464 (E7) resp. 1694 (E8) weak-order suffixes V is 6, attained only by J=(0,1,3,5,n-1); no suffix has a 3-path in its left descent set. Consistency: part_b3_chains.py gives maximum 5 (J=(1,3,4,6)) for Gern's w6, matching a(w6)=5 from Gern Prop. 4.5.1 and the nonzero mu(x6,w6)=1; and 3 for w4, matching a(w4)=3. The ingredients are Gern's Prop. 1.3.5 (deg P_{e,w} <= (l(w)-a(w))/2) and Prop. 1.2.7(1)/Lemma 2.3.9 (P_{x_n,w_n} = P_{e,w_n}) at sources/gern-thesis-2013.txt lines 580-583 and 1492-1497.
  FIX: Add one sentence after the parity step: 'For the two full-support terminals the vanishing does not depend on parity: each has a reduced factorization through w_0(J), J=I u {0} of type A2 x A1^3, so a(b) >= 6 > |I|+1 and Lusztig's bound deg P_{e,b} <= (l(b)-a(b))/2 together with P_{x,b}=P_{e,b} gives mu(x,b)=0.'

--- [note|unverified-minor|conf=high] followup-new-terminals-structure-D5: E_n analogue of Gern's Theorem 2.3.6 holds in ranks 6-8 and could be stated as a proposition
  LOC: results/exceptional-leading.tex lines 277-281 (Proposition prop:classification)
  CLAIM: Independent recomputation shows that in E6, E7, E8 there is a unique longest noncommuting terminal (lengths 7, 28, 50), and every noncommuting terminal is b_k * u with b_4=w4 (l=7), b_6=w6 (l=15), b_7 (l=28), b_8 (l=50), k<=n, and u a product of commuting generators neither in nor adjacent to supp(b_k) (only u=s5 in E7 and u=s5 or s6 in E8, all on w4). The length-28 and length-50 elements are therefore maximal-length among all terminals of their type, and Proposition prop:classification could be phrased in Gern's form.

Non-adjacent complements of supp(w4)={1,2,3,n-1}: {5} in E7, {5,6} in E8 (5~6), matching the observed u.
  FIX: Restate Proposition prop:classification as: 'For n=6,7,8 the noncommuting terminals of E_n are exactly the elements b_k u, k in {4,6,7,8}, k<=n, where b_4,b_6 are Gern's w4,w6 and b_7,b_8 are the elements of length 28 and 50, and u is a commuting product of generators not in or adjacent to supp(b_k). In particular b_n is the unique longest noncommuting terminal.'

VERIFIED_OK:
  + Gern's bracket form (Lemma 2.3.4, Definition 2.3.1) reproduces the signed permutations of Corollary 2.2.19 and the lengths 3n^2/8+n/4 of Lemma 2.2.20 for n=4..10 (part_a_gern_rows.py).
  + The label map 'Gern 1,...,6 -> 1,n-1,2,3,4,5' in the proof of Theorem thm:main sends w6 to the E7 row 132543621324356 and the E8 row 132543721324357, and x6=s1 s2 s4 s6 to 1356/1357; the displayed signed permutations x6=(-1,-2,4,3,6,5), w6=(-1,-6,3,-4,5,-2) are correct.
  + All nine non-new Table 1 rows are Gern elements under the label map (exact matrix equality), and the E8 length-28 row equals the E7 length-28 row with 6 relabelled 7.
  + Exhaustive enumeration of D7 as even signed permutations (322,560 elements) gives exactly the bad elements w4, w4 s6, w4 s7, w6, confirming Gern Theorem 2.3.6 for n=5,6,7 and the counts 1/3/4 of Gern rows in E6/E7/E8.
  + Both new words are reduced (inversion count equals word length 28 resp. 50), two-sided terminal by the root test (eq. terminaltest), with L=R={1,3,5,6} resp. {1,3,5,7} and full support, as stated in Table 1; gaps 24 and 46 are even as used in the proof.
  + Independence numbers 3,4,4,4,4 of D4, D4+A1, D6, E7, E8 quoted in the proof of Corollary cor:eligible are correct.
  + Independent brute force over all of E6 and E7 reproduces the paper's counts: 576 E7 right-terminals, 22/36 commuting terminals, 1/4 noncommuting terminals with exactly the table words; group orders 51,840 and 2,903,040 and maximal lengths 36 and 63.
  + Independent one-step parabolic pruning in E8 (240 x 576 = 138,240 candidates, matching the paper's 'final stage' count) reproduces 2,160 right-terminals, 64 two-sided terminals, 58 commuting and the six noncommuting table words.
  + Lemma lem:pruning (right-terminality of w=av passes to v in W_J) is correct; I re-proved it and relied on it in e8_pruning.py.
  + The length-28 and length-50 elements are the unique longest noncommuting terminals in E7 and E8 (hence maximal-length among terminals of their type).
  + Both new elements are involutions (w^2=1) with (-1)-eigenspaces of dimensions 4 and 2, equal to products of 4 resp. 2 mutually orthogonal reflections; the fixed-root subsystems are 3A1 resp. D6.
  + The w_0(J)-factor lower bound for the a-function reproduces Gern's a(w4)=3 and a(w6)=5 (Prop. 4.5.1), a consistency check of the method used in finding D4.

UNRESOLVED:
  ? Exact Lusztig a-values and the polynomials P_{e,b} for the two new elements were not computed (only the lower bound a(b) >= 6 from a w_0(A2 x A1^3) factor); a KL computation for a length-28 element in E7 or length-50 in E8 was out of scope for Python here.
  ? The fine conjugacy-class label of the E7 element (4A1' versus 4A1'' in Carter's notation) was determined only up to 'not a D4 frame'; I did not cross-check against Carter's tables.
  ? I did not re-implement the paper's full recursive pruning chain E8 > E7 > ... > {e} or the D7-coset method; my E8 verification uses a single pruning step through the E7 parabolic with my own brute-force E7 right-terminal list.
  ? Claims outside this dimension (FC catalogues, star-reduction lemma, uniform and affine families, D6 KL certificate) were not re-verified here.
